- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. ([patryksztuczka/boberstack](https://github.com/patryksztuczka/boberstack/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★2)

- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same check: write the premise down and test it before a third fix. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★1)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same check, such as a metric that will not move, a pipeline that keeps failing one assertion, or a model score that stays flat. Write the premise down and test it directly before you try another fix. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★1)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when repeated fixes fail and a shared assumption may be wrong. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when fixes sharing one premise keep failing a gate. ([nodecode-ai/cells](https://github.com/nodecode-ai/cells/tree/HEAD/tools/nodecode-pstack/prose/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★1)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: When two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate, suspect the premise, not the fixes. ([svssdeva/agentic-skills](https://github.com/svssdeva/agentic-skills/tree/HEAD/engineering/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★1)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: 同じ前提に立つ修正が 2 回以上、同じ検査で落ちたとき、または直した部分に次の穴が出たときに適用する。次の修正を書く前に、前提を 1 文に書き出して直接確かめる。 ([B16B1RD/bb-writ](https://github.com/B16B1RD/bb-writ/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Fixes sharing one premise keep failing the same gate. Test the premise first. ([blackviperxiii-ui/vstack](https://github.com/blackviperxiii-ui/vstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. - **principle-attack-the-premise**: 하나의 전제를 공유하는 둘 이상의 수정이 동일한 게이트에서 실패했을 때 적용. 전제를 기록하고 그것을 가정하는 또 다른 수정을 작성하기 전에 직접 테스트. ([lee-chance/ai-skills-ko](https://github.com/lee-chance/ai-skills-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack-ko/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Nội quy: khi 2 lần sửa trở lên cùng dựa trên một giả định đều fail cùng một cổng, viết giả định đó ra và kiểm chứng trực tiếp nó trước khi viết thêm bản sửa dựa trên nó. ([lichihouse/claude-plugins](https://github.com/lichihouse/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Question the shared premise before the next fix. - **principle-attack-the-premise**: 전제 하나를 공유하는 수정 두 개 이상이 같은 게이트에서 실패했을 때 적용한다. 그 전제를 깔고 가는 수정을 또 쓰기 전에 전제를 적고 직접 검증한다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))

- **principle-author-is-not-approver**: Apply to every test, test case, plan, or verdict you produced. A draft is done only after a fresh-context reviewer checks it and after you see each new test fail against a broken version. Your own review, passing tests, and CI are not the independent check. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-author-is-not-approver) · ★1)
- **principle-automate-mechanics**: Apply across workflows when choosing between agent judgment, existing tools, and saved scripts. Proactively capture stable, repeatable mechanics as verified automation within the requested scope. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-automate-mechanics))
- **principle-avoid-tautological-tests**: Apply when writing or reviewing automated tests, regression tests, expected values, fixtures, mocks, snapshots, property tests, or coverage-driven test additions. Tautological tests are harmful: require an independent test oracle and a credible way for each test to fail under a plausible defect; reject assertions that merely restate production logic, fixture setup, mock configuration, or the value under test. ([dakdevs/skills](https://github.com/dakdevs/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-avoid-tautological-tests))
- **principle-aws-architecture**: AWS で構成を設計、レビューするときの原則。Well-Architected Framework を土台に、復旧の目標と DR、アカウントの分け方、ネットワーク、ID と権限、データの保護、記録と検知、後から変えられない決定を扱う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-aws-architecture) · ★10)
- **principle-backend-api-contract**: Apply when adding or changing a backend endpoint, or an asynchronous event/message. Write or update the OpenAPI (sync) or AsyncAPI (async) contract first and generate server types/stubs from it, instead of writing the handler or publisher/consumer freehand. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-api-contract) · ★4)
- **principle-backend-compat**: Apply when changing an existing endpoint's request/response shape, an existing event/message schema, or a database migration. Default to additive changes; version or deprecate anything that would break an existing caller, consumer, or deployed row instead of changing it in place. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-compat) · ★4)
- **principle-backend-layering**: Apply before adding business logic to a handler/route, direct DB/ORM/HTTP access outside a repository or external wrapper, or any code that would duplicate a transport/business-logic/data-access split the project already uses elsewhere. Detect whether that split is already established; if it is, match it. If it isn't, say so and skip rather than scaffolding layers a small project hasn't earned yet. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-layering) · ★4)
- **principle-backend-performance**: Apply when adding a hot request path, a goroutine/worker/background job, or anything that can block a request thread or the event loop. Benchmark before/after on a representative load, bound concurrency instead of spawning it unbounded, and don't block the request path on work that doesn't need to finish before the response. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-performance) · ★4)
- **principle-backend-security**: Apply when adding or touching a request/event boundary, an authn/authz decision, a secret, or a third-party dependency in a backend service. Validate at the edge, enforce the specific least-privilege decision, never hardcode a secret, use parameterized queries, and check new dependencies for known vulnerabilities. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-security) · ★4)
- **principle-behavioral-substitutability**: Preserve supported behavior when implementations, inheritance, extension contracts, or mutable domain invariants change. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-behavioral-substitutability) · ★1)
- **principle-behavioral-verification**: Express API and function intent as executable guarantees through the owning boundary; use when writing tests or assessing regression evidence. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-behavioral-verification) · ★1)
- **principle-bind-assigned-ports**: Apply whenever starting a server, choosing a port, or handing back a URL. Ports are assigned, never chosen. Bind 127.0.0.1 explicitly. Anything the operator opens must be forwarded. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-bind-assigned-ports))
- **principle-bind-to-named-inventory**: Apply after an audit, scoring review, or a request for a "real classifier" / "less arbitrary weights" when existing scorers, rankers, or weight formulas were already named. - **principle-block-on-the-human**: Apply when an open question could be answered by the person in front of you. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-block-on-the-human))
- **principle-bound-retries**: Make retries safe, classified, budgeted, and capacity-aware across every execution layer. - **principle-bounded-repair**: Bound every repair loop — retrying an identical failure the same way burns budget without generating information. Set a repair budget before fixing, change the hypothesis or the observation when a retry fails the same way twice, and escalate to a blocker report when the budget expires instead of weakening acceptance. Apply during any fix loop, CI-chasing, or repeated verification failure. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-bounded-repair) · ★3)
- **principle-bug-fixing**: バグを修正するときに、症状を抑えるパッチではなく根本の原因を直すためのスキル。原因を特定し、同じ原因を持つ箇所を洗い出し、再現テストで原因を捉えてから、原因を取り除く最小の差分で直す。 コードの不具合の修正を求められたときは、依頼がテストや原因に触れていなくても必ずこのスキルを使うこと。 「バグ直して」「不具合を修正して」「〜になってしまう、修正お願い」「エラーが出るので直して」「問い合わせがあった件を直して」といった依頼で使う。原因の調査だけを頼まれた場合にも使う（コードやテストは変更せず、原因と同じ原因を持つ箇所を報告する）。 ([kazukit-dev/dotfiles](https://github.com/kazukit-dev/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/skills/principle-bug-fixing))

([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-central-provider-wiring) · ★1)

- **principle-challenge-shared-premise**: Question the premise shared by repeatedly failing fixes and choose an observation that can disprove it before another attempt. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-challenge-shared-premise) · ★1)
- **principle-challenge-the-brief**: Apply when a founder states who their customer is, what works, or why deals happen. Check claims against evidence instead of writing them down. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-challenge-the-brief) · ★3)
- **principle-change-sync**: Repeatable workflow for synchronizing APT principle updates across doctrine, checklists, prompts, and references. Use when a principle changes and linked enforcement artifacts must be updated consistently. Triggers: principle update, terminology change, checklist drift, prompt drift, reference contract alignment. ([adthomps/apt-principles-agents](https://github.com/adthomps/apt-principles-agents/tree/HEAD/platforms/github-copilot/source/skills/principle-change-sync))
- **principle-chase-y-not-x**: Apply when a request arrives already shaped as a solution (X): find the real need (Y) behind it before building, and propose a better X when one serves Y. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-chase-y-not-x) · ★1)
- **principle-cheapest-layer-that-catches-it**: Apply when you choose where a test lives. Put each check at the lowest layer where you can observe the failure: static, unit, component or contract, API or integration, end-to-end, then manual or exploratory. Do not repeat one assertion across layers. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cheapest-layer-that-catches-it) · ★1)
- **principle-check-the-facts**: Use before writing assumptions, a spec, a design or a rollout plan: never build on a business fact nobody confirmed. Lists the business facts a decision rests on and sources each from CLAUDE.md, .fbs/context.md or the user; asks the missing or stale ones. ([fabricio21x/fbs](https://github.com/fabricio21x/fbs/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-check-the-facts) · ★1)
- **principle-claims-rest-on-records**: Apply before stating any number, status, or citation to the supervisor, when judging a subagent's or tool's report, and when a result looks too good. ([tosunlab/labstack](https://github.com/tosunlab/labstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-claims-rest-on-records) · ★1)
- **principle-classify-before-you-fix**: Apply when a test fails. Name the failure class with evidence before you change anything: product regression, test defect, contract or doc drift, environment, data collision, flaky, or known issue. Do not edit a test or expected value to match the observed behavior unless the class is test defect and the oracle source agrees. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-classify-before-you-fix) · ★1)
- **principle-clean-architecture**: Clean Architecture, hexagonal architecture, ports and adapters, and dependency rule. Auto-load when designing architecture, choosing layers, ports, adapters, or keeping the domain free of framework code. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clean-architecture) · ★3)
- **principle-clean-architecture-dotnet**: The layered architecture for a .NET system of this class — Domain / Application / Infrastructure with thin composition-root hosts (API, Worker, UI). Defines dependency direction, what each layer may reference, the patterns this standard deliberately omits, and how SOLID is applied. Use when placing a new type, adding a project reference, or deciding where a concern belongs. ([oskarc/AssetHub](https://github.com/oskarc/AssetHub/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-clean-architecture-dotnet) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-clean-code**: Clean code, DRY, KISS, YAGNI, function length, abstraction level, function argument count, Boy Scout Rule, intent-revealing naming. Auto-load when writing functions, reviewing code clarity, or debating whether to abstract. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clean-code) · ★3)
- **principle-clean-up-what-you-started**: WHEN tearing down after a run, verification or failed attempt; NOT for making reruns converge or reverting unrelated edits; releases only resources this run created and keeps the evidence. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-clean-up-what-you-started) · ★37)
- **principle-clear-naming**: Apply when naming variables, functions, types, files, or endpoints. Enforces the 6-month amnesia test, language conventions, intent-based naming, and cross-boundary consistency. ([Scino/fstack](https://github.com/Scino/fstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clear-naming) · ★13 · 2 copies)
- **principle-close-or-block-the-dimension**: Apply at the end of any planning session. Every dimension ends either settled or as a named decision ticket - never merely discussed. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-close-or-block-the-dimension))
- **principle-code-comment**: コードのコメントに何を書き、何を書かないかを決めるスキル。コメントには原則として Why not（素直な書き方や代案を採らなかった理由）だけを書き、How はコード、What はテスト、Why はコミットメッセージに任せる。 コードを書く・編集するとき、コメントを書く・直す・消すとき、コメントや JSDoc をレビューするとき、「コメント足して」「コメントいる？」「このコメントどう？」と言われたときは必ずこのスキルを使うこと。テストコードのコメントにも使う。 ([kazukit-dev/dotfiles](https://github.com/kazukit-dev/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/skills/principle-code-comment))
- **principle-code-first-documentation**: Treat generated OpenAPI and its documentation UI as part of every HTTP boundary. Mandatory when creating, changing, reviewing, or diagnosing an HTTP server, route, handler, request or response schema, or API error contract. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-code-first-documentation) · ★1)
- **principle-code-quality**: Load before writing or changing code in any language, and before reviewing a diff, designing types or interfaces, refactoring, or debugging. Covers cross-language limits, naming, code smells, type and boundary discipline, domain modelling, reader load, deletion-first sequencing, and scope rules, plus on-demand references for Python, TypeScript, C#, GDScript, and Godot. ([Uraxii/dotai](https://github.com/Uraxii/dotai/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-code-quality))
- **principle-code-review**: Code review heuristics — four-axis review lens (correctness, security, design integrity, test coverage); confidence-based filtering (no finding without a concrete failure scenario); review comment tone (observation over accusation); nitpick filtering; what counts as a real finding vs linter noise. Auto-load when writing or framing a review comment, deciding whether a PR finding is worth surfacing, reviewing a diff for correctness, or filtering review nitpicks. ([aibot88/sec_skill_store](https://github.com/aibot88/sec_skill_store/tree/HEAD/skills/claudskills/principle-code-review) · ★6)
- **principle-code-review**: Code review heuristics — five-axis review lens (correctness, security, design integrity, test coverage, comment quality); review comment tone (observation over accusation); nitpick filtering; distinguishing a real finding from linter noise. Auto-load when writing or framing a review comment, reviewing a diff for correctness, or filtering review nitpicks. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-code-review) · ★3)
- **principle-code-speaks-for-itself**: Apply whenever you write or edit code. Never add a comment that explains the code or your work on it. Say it with names, types, smaller functions, and tests; your work goes in the commit message and the review. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-code-speaks-for-itself))
- **principle-coding**: 設計・コード・テスト・変更の進め方で判断に迷ったときに参照する、名前で通じる原則の一覧と、原則どうしがぶつかったときの優先順位。コードやテストを書くとき、レビューするときに使う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-coding) · ★10)
- **principle-column**: Write a short opinion column that names a trend the reader has already noticed, grants it, flips it in one short sentence, defines a principle in two plain sentences, concedes that many reject it, and closes on an assertion plus one hedge. Use this skill for product-management and leadership columns, newsletter opinion pieces, "my take on X" posts, principle explainers, and any 600 to 1,700 word piece for executives and practitioners deciding how to organise; also when the user asks for "a column", "a short opinion piece", "a hot take that still holds up", or "explain the principle behind X".… ([3orionkings/writing-skills](https://github.com/3orionkings/writing-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/writing-skills/skills/principle-column) · ★2)
- **principle-comments-as-design**: Use comments as a design tool to capture abstractions, intent, and decisions code can't express. Use when designing a module, interface, or data structure whose intent won't be obvious from the code alone, and when the user asks to "add comments", "document this", or "explain this code". ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-comments-as-design))
- **principle-communication**: Caveman output mode — ultra-terse brief replies with fewer tokens. Strips filler, hedging, pleasantries. Three levels — caveman lite (default), caveman full, ultra caveman / max caveman (maximum compression). Persists until normal mode. Auto-load when the user requests terser output, says "be brief", "less tokens", "caveman mode", "use fewer tokens", or invokes `/caveman`. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-communication) · ★3)
- **principle-completion-evidence**: Before claiming completion, directly verify the final artifact or requested behavior and show the evidence supporting that claim. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-completion-evidence) · ★1)
- **principle-compose-vue-components**: Apply when building or refactoring Vue components. Design accessibility first, organize by responsibility, and express layout variants through composition. ([alexanderop/pstack](https://github.com/alexanderop/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-compose-vue-components) · ★1)
- **principle-composition-and-mechanisms**: Keep feature policy at its composition point and extract mechanisms only for independent obligations; use for assembly and interface boundaries. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-composition-and-mechanisms) · ★1)
- **principle-composition-over-flags**: Apply when a component, function or API grows boolean props or mode flags: split it into primitives that compose instead. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-composition-over-flags) · ★1)
- **principle-concise**: Apply to any prose you produce: replies, PR descriptions, commit messages, docs, agent-facing instructions. Short declarative sentences, no filler, no bloat; every sentence earns its place by changing a decision. ([RestartDK/dotfiles](https://github.com/RestartDK/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/agents/skills/dstack/principle-concise) · ★1)
- **principle-concurrency**: Concurrency principles — race conditions, livelock, backpressure, lock-free primitives, choosing between mutex vs channel vs actor vs semaphore vs CAS. Auto-load when designing concurrent code, fixing a deadlock, choosing a synchronization primitive, or reasoning about goroutine/thread/task lifetimes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-concurrency) · ★3)
- **principle-confidence-from-many-perspectives**: Apply when you are about to call a design, plan, ticket or root-cause claim done, or a change that would be costly if wrong. Ask how confident you are it will work, and get that confidence from independent reviews, not from your own certainty. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-confidence-from-many-perspectives))
- **principle-conflict**: Run CLAUDE.md's Principle Conflict Protocol when applying an Engineering Principle would conflict with an existing rule, established pattern, or current implementation — including a violation discovered mid-task. Stops work, explains the conflict, and offers three resolution paths for the developer to choose. Use the moment a conflict is noticed, not after picking a side. ([Bluecode77732/Upload-Board-Project](https://github.com/Bluecode77732/Upload-Board-Project/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-conflict) · ★1)
- **principle-constraint-moves**: Apply after relief work ships, a checkpoint fires, or metrics shift. Re-identify the binding constraint. Yesterday's bottleneck is not permanently sacred. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-constraint-moves) · ★6)
- **principle-contain-untrusted-work**: Treat lower-trust data as data and restrict the authority and resources of code, parsers, plugins, tools, and workers that process it. Mandatory when lower-trust input is interpreted or executed, selects a path, URL, query, or command, or receives access to files, networks, secrets, tenants, or sensitive effects. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-contain-untrusted-work) · ★1)
- **principle-contextualize-and-write-for-the-reader**: Contextualize and write for the reader. Apply when writing anything someone else will read later: a design, a handoff, findings, a report, a verification, a brief, a reply. ([erickb336/sage-stack](https://github.com/erickb336/sage-stack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sage-codex/skills/principle-contextualize-and-write-for-the-reader) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-coordinate-external-effects**: Move durable intent across transaction and ownership boundaries through explicit delivery, reconciliation, and compensation protocols. Mandatory when committed local work must call a provider, publish a message, control a device, send a notification, or otherwise change an external system. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-coordinate-external-effects) · ★1)
- **principle-corrections-become-tests**: Apply whenever a human corrects a label, draft, score or claim. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-corrections-become-tests) · ★3)
- **principle-correctness-and-failure-paths**: Trace correctness and failure paths; preserve error context and recovery ownership when logic, guards, errors, or fallbacks change. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-correctness-and-failure-paths) · ★1)
- **principle-correctness-before-speed**: Apply before you time, report, or keep any kernel change. A perf number counts only after an allclose check and a NaN count pass on the same run. ([anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack](https://github.com/anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-correctness-before-speed) · ★1)
- **principle-correctness-over-pragmatism**: Apply when tempted by a shortcut (a cast, a stub, a guard that masks a crash, a downgraded plan): take the structurally correct fix unless the owner explicitly asked for speed. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-correctness-over-pragmatism) · ★1)
- **principle-cost-aware-delegation**: Apply when delegating to subagents, CI or paid APIs: budget turns and scope before the handoff, front-load context, cap scope, and commit passing work before the budget runs out. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cost-aware-delegation) · ★1)
- **principle-cost-awareness**: Cost awareness principles — FinOps mindset, right-sizing, cost-per-request model, storage tier selection, cardinality explosion, log volume. Auto-load when reasoning about cloud costs, right-sizing instances, scale-to-zero trade-offs, or FinOps budget constraints. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cost-awareness) · ★3)
- **principle-creation**: Extract reusable decision-making principles from experiences - especially failures - to build a personal or organizational repository of codified learning. ([sethmblack/paks-skills](https://github.com/sethmblack/paks-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-creation) · ★10)
- **principle-customer-data-stays-home**: Apply before any list of people or companies goes to an outside tool, such as an enrichment tool, a scraper or a sender. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-customer-data-stays-home) · ★3)
- **principle-data-modeling**: Data modeling — relational vs document vs KV vs graph selection, normalization depth, indexing strategy, hot-key avoidance, schema evolution via expand–contract, query-first design, retention and archival. Auto-load when designing schemas, schema evolution, indexing strategy, hot-key avoidance, query-first design, or data retention. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-data-modeling) · ★3)
- **principle-ddd**: Domain-Driven Design (DDD) — bounded contexts, entities, domain events, and repository pattern. Auto-load when modeling a complex domain, microservice splits, designing aggregates, or aligning code with business language. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-ddd) · ★3)
- **principle-decision-trail**: Record consequential choices with evidence during long-running, unattended, or multi-session work; preserve pivots without inventing history. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-decision-trail) · ★1)
- **principle-decision-trail**: Apply to long, autonomous, or high-stakes work, and whenever a JEV decision changed the course. Keep a reviewable record of decisions, confidences, and sources. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-decision-trail))
- **principle-deep-module-design**: Design modules with simple interfaces and rich implementations. Use when creating, extending, or refactoring any module, class, service or API. Trigger on phrases like "simplify the interface", "reduce API surface", "encapsulate", "clean up boundaries", "split this class", "extract a helper", "wrap this", or before writing the first line of a new abstraction. ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-deep-module-design))
- **principle-deep-modules**: Judge module depth by useful behavior versus caller knowledge and duties; use when evaluating interfaces, hidden mechanics, or seams. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-deep-modules) · ★1)
- **principle-defensive-history**: Recover the incident or contract behind unclear defensive code before removing it; missing rationale is not proof it is unnecessary. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-defensive-history) · ★1)
- **principle-define-done**: WHEN starting a multi-step deliverable, phase or plan, or deciding whether one is finished; NOT for single-step answers or choosing which checks to run; sets a checkable completion criterion first and reports every unmet gate. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-define-done) · ★37)
- **principle-define-errors-away**: Remove error conditions by redesigning the API instead of handling them. Use when designing an API's error surface, when error handling in a module is getting dense, or when the user asks to "simplify error handling" or "reduce exceptions". ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-define-errors-away))
- **principle-define-the-domain-first**: Use when a new noun, state, or stored field appears, one word is doing two jobs, or two sources use different words for what may be one thing. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-define-the-domain-first))
- **principle-define-the-question**: Apply at the start of an analysis, before writing the first query. Write down the decision the analysis serves, the metric definition, the population, the time window, and the result that would change the decision. Without this, analysis drifts into fishing. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-define-the-question) · ★1)
- **principle-delegate**: How to spawn and trust subagents. Use before delegating work, and when a subagent's report comes back. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-delegate) · ★1)
- **principle-delegate-after-the-contract**: Assess delegation when work could use separate contexts, parallel execution, or a less capable model. ([BumpyClock/agent-templates](https://github.com/BumpyClock/agent-templates/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack/principle-delegate-after-the-contract) · ★2)
- **principle-delegate-implementation-review-stays-here**: Apply when handing work to a subagent. Implementation delegates; design, review, verification, and git mutations stay with the lead. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-delegate-implementation-review-stays-here))
- **principle-deliver-and-prove**: Establish readiness to deliver an implementation and verify its expected behavior before editing. Use when beginning implementation or when new findings change what delivery or proof requires. ([ahmadabdalla/aasystem](https://github.com/ahmadabdalla/aasystem/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-deliver-and-prove) · ★1)
- **principle-depend-on-caller-contracts**: Apply when you design a dependency or interface, or call through a collaborator to reach another object. ([nilpath/nilpath-marketplace](https://github.com/nilpath/nilpath-marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/plugins/nstack/skills/principle-depend-on-caller-contracts) · 3 copies)
- **principle-dependencies**: Dependencies that point one way, onto the language's own tools and stable public APIs, one home for each library, the runtimes a project targets, floating ranges with a pinned lockfile, and deciding logic kept pure. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-dependencies))
- **principle-derive-dont-sync**: Apply when about to store a value that could be computed from existing state, or to keep two copies of one truth in step: derive from one source instead. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-derive-dont-sync) · ★1)
- **principle-derive-from-one-owner**: WHEN the same fact or decision is computed or stored in more than one place; NOT for modelling legal states or collapsing pass-through layers; gives it one owner that other sites derive from or receive. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-derive-from-one-owner) · ★37)
- **principle-design**: A design built from proven patterns and SOLID objects that put behaviour with the data it serves, short because it fits the problem. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-design))
- **principle-design-for-concurrency**: Use when actors already overlap: two workers, retries, callbacks, background tasks, or late results can touch the same resource. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-design-for-concurrency))
- **principle-design-for-failure**: Use when a call, job, or state change has an effect someone else can see and it can time out, be retried, partly succeed, or end without you knowing. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-design-for-failure))
- **principle-design-for-operations**: Keeps software correct and identical wherever it runs: crash-only design (fails, stops halfway, runs twice, runs concurrently), idempotent jobs, append-only data runs, and twelve-factor config, logs and releases. Use when writing jobs, pipelines, ETL, workers, CLIs, retries, config, env vars, secrets, logging, deployment or releases. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-for-operations) · ★1)
- **principle-design-foundations**: Resolve competing design criteria from the current use case, trusted values, ownership, and concrete costs rather than a fixed architecture. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-design-foundations) · ★1)
- **principle-design-harness**: AI エージェントに UI を作らせても品質がぶれないよう、制約・文脈・検証・フィードバックの 4 層で作業環境を整えるための原則。こぎそ氏が提唱した「デザインハーネス」に基づく。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-design-harness) · ★10)
- **principle-design-patterns**: Design patterns — Gang of Four and beyond. Strategy, factory method, observer, decorator, adapter, facade, template method, command, repository, dependency injection. Auto-load when designing class structure, refactoring toward a pattern, or evaluating whether a pattern applies. ([aibot88/sec_skill_store](https://github.com/aibot88/sec_skill_store/tree/HEAD/skills/claudskills/principle-design-patterns) · ★6)
- **principle-design-patterns**: Design patterns — Gang of Four and beyond. Strategy, factory method, observer, adapter, template method, dependency injection. Auto-load when designing class structure, or evaluating whether a pattern applies. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-patterns) · ★3)
- **principle-design-system**: デザインシステムを作る、直す、使うときに、それぞれの要素がどうあるべきかを判断するための原則。基本原則、部品とパターン、トークン、運用の観点。Alla Kholmatova『Design Systems』をはじめ、広く参照される文献に基づく。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-design-system) · ★10)
- **principle-design-system-first**: Apply to any UI work. Start from the app's existing design system, tokens, and primitives; a new primitive needs justification. Sweat transitions, alignment, and error states; every feature earns its place. ([RestartDK/dotfiles](https://github.com/RestartDK/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/agents/skills/dstack/principle-design-system-first) · ★1)
- **principle-design-the-core**: Encodes rules in the shape of the data and keeps them in a pure core: sum types, state machines, tables, value objects, parsing at the edge, I/O in thin adapters, ports only where something really varies. Use when choosing types, schemas or data structures, placing new logic, or wiring a database, API, CLI, queue or LLM client. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-the-core) · ★1)
- **principle-design-to-the-port-not-the-vendor**: Apply when writing or changing a port's contract, adding a product or a delivery mechanism behind one, naming a config table or glossary term after a product, or writing code that branches on which product or source is in use. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-to-the-port-not-the-vendor))
- **principle-deterministic-tests**: Apply to any test that uses time, randomness, ordering, network, concurrency, or asynchronous UI. The same code and the same inputs must give the same result. Wait on conditions. Do not use fixed sleeps. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-deterministic-tests) · ★1)
- **principle-deterministic-tests**: Tests that each fail when, and only when, the behaviour they guard breaks. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-deterministic-tests))
- **principle-discomfort-is-a-signal**: Apply when the named constraint feels easy or flattering, or when the owner tours opportunities and activity lists. Prefer the uncomfortable answer. Activity lists are the default dodge. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-discomfort-is-a-signal) · ★6)
- **principle-distributed-systems**: Distributed systems principles — CAP, consistency models (linearizable, causal, eventual, read-your-writes), quorum, leader election, partitioning, idempotency across nodes, fallacies of distributed computing. Auto-load when reasoning about CAP/PACELC trade-offs, choosing a consistency model, ordering events with logical clocks, distinguishing exactly-once delivery from exactly-once effects, designing replication or partitioning strategy, or assessing distributed failure modes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-distributed-systems) · ★3)
- **principle-divide-the-work**: Divide the work when a task splits into independent parts, such as several files, sources, or checks whose results do not depend on each other, or when one step would flood the main thread with bulk output. Give each part to its own subagent and merge short summaries. ([ChandlerWeeks/chandler-skills](https://github.com/ChandlerWeeks/chandler-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-divide-the-work))
- **principle-documentation**: ドキュメントに何を載せ、何を載せないかの原則と事例。リポジトリのドキュメント、エージェント向けの指示ファイル、コードコメントを書くとき、見直すときに使う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-documentation) · ★10)
- **principle-dont-invent-problems**: Applies when a whats-going-on stage decides whether a concern is a problem, whether to classify it, or how sure to be. Do not invent a problem the input does not support. ([unfriction-ai/whats-going-on](https://github.com/unfriction-ai/whats-going-on/tree/HEAD/plugins/whats-going-on/skills/whats-going-on/principle-dont-invent-problems) · 2 copies)
- **principle-dont-solve-yet**: Applies when a whats-going-on stage is about to diagnose, brainstorm, or recommend. Stop once the problem is identified. ([unfriction-ai/whats-going-on](https://github.com/unfriction-ai/whats-going-on/tree/HEAD/plugins/whats-going-on/skills/whats-going-on/principle-dont-solve-yet) · 2 copies)
- **principle-dry**: One home for every piece of knowledge. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-dry))
- **principle-durable-loop**: Apply when the outcome should continue with no person feeding the next task. Build the access first. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-durable-loop))
- **principle-earn-every-element**: WHEN adding UI controls, settings, options, API parameters or document sections; NOT observing a user journey or weighing scope trade-offs; keeps only elements with a stated user need. - **principle-enforce-access-explicitly**: Apply to any data read or write path. CRUD, field-level security, and sharing are deliberate and stated: with sharing on the class, WITH USER_MODE or Security.stripInaccessible() on the data path, and a written reason for every without sharing or system-mode exception. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-enforce-access-explicitly) · ★1)
- **principle-enforce-distributed-authority**: Prevent stale or competing workers from committing protected changes through atomic claims, conditional writes, and fencing. Mandatory when work can move between workers, leases expire, leadership changes, or failover can leave an earlier actor running. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-enforce-distributed-authority) · ★1)
- **principle-enforce-idempotent-effects**: Make equivalent requests converge on one logical effect through receiver-side identity and atomic arbitration. Mandatory when a command or external side effect may be delivered or invoked more than once. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-enforce-idempotent-effects) · ★1)
- **principle-enforce-where-it-is-stored**: Apply when more than one client (a web app, an API caller, an agent or MCP tool, an import job) can write the same data, or when a client fills in or corrects a field before sending it. The system that stores the data enforces its rules for every writer; a client that covers for a missing server rule hides the gap. - **principle-engagement-is-not-consent**: Apply when a signal such as a like, comment, follow or visit is used as a reason to reach out or to keep data. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-engagement-is-not-consent) · ★3)
- **principle-error-design**: Apply when adding or changing an error: its type, message, public shape, status code or retry guidance. Say what failed and why, end on a way forward, carry typed context, keep reassurance provable, and translate each failure deliberately at the boundary. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-error-design))
- **principle-error-handling**: Error handling principles — errors as values vs exceptions, wrapping and context chains, retry with exponential backoff and jitter, circuit breakers and bulkheads, log-once boundary discipline. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-error-handling) · ★3)
- **principle-event-driven**: Event-driven architecture — event sourcing, CQRS, sagas, schema evolution, consumer groups, partitions, idempotent handlers, outbox pattern, dead letter queues. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-event-driven) · ★3)
- **principle-evidence-before-verdicts**: Apply when investigating, reviewing, or reporting conclusions. Support verdicts with inspected evidence and distinguish defects from uncertainty and preferences. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-evidence-before-verdicts))
- **principle-evidence-in-every-claim**: Apply to every report, reply, PR body, and review. Each claim carries its evidence or its label (measured, inferred, or guess) in the same sentence. - **principle-evidence-not-claims**: Apply before you report any test status, coverage, fix, or readiness. A verdict must rest on an executed run against the artifact under test, quoted and rerunnable. Proxies such as collect-only, compiling, a dry run, or green CI on another commit are NOT RUN. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-evidence-not-claims) · ★1)
- **principle-evidence-over-report**: When a report and the repository disagree — a worker claims success with no commit, a tracker says done but the gate is red — the repo is right and the report is narrative. Apply whenever consuming status from tickets, workers, comments, dashboards, or pipeline state. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-evidence-over-report) · ★3)
- **principle-evolve-and-restore-state**: Keep durable data, messages, work, authority, and external effects safe across mixed-version deployment, rollback, migration, failover, backup, and restore. Mandatory when changing a durable representation or protocol, running mixed versions, resuming old work, or designing rollback, migration, backup, restore, or failover. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-evolve-and-restore-state) · ★1)
- **principle-excuse-first**: Apply before writing any outreach. Every message needs an honest, recent, relevant reason to exist. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-excuse-first) · ★3)
- **principle-exhaust-design-space**: Apply when a novel interaction or architecture decision has no useful precedent. Build two or three competing prototypes and compare them before committing. ([ImAno177/cstack](https://github.com/ImAno177/cstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-exhaust-design-space))

- **principle-finish-or-report**: Apply at the end of any run. Never deliver a partial silently. Either the whole thing is done, or say precisely what is left and why. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-finish-or-report))

- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Reproduce the symptom, trace causality until one mechanism explains it, and fix that mechanism rather than compensating for downstream effects. Use when during debugging and incident repair. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★117 · 2 copies)

- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. ([patryksztuczka/boberstack](https://github.com/patryksztuczka/boberstack/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★2)

- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 디버깅할 때 적용한다. 각 증상을 시스템이 처음 잘못된 곳까지 추적해 거기서 고친다. 방어 코드는 그것이 다루는 상태가 정당할 때만 수정이다. ([yjb94/jbstack](https://github.com/yjb94/jbstack/tree/HEAD/core/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★2)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging or fixing a bug: fix the first place the system went wrong, not where the symptom surfaced, and every place with the same fault. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Use when fixing a bug or a test that fails unexpectedly: reproduce it first, trace the symptom to its cause, and fix the cause rather than the place the symptom appeared. ([fabricio21x/fbs](https://github.com/fabricio21x/fbs/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a wrong number, a failing pipeline, a broken notebook, or a failing test. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when a workaround would hide a defect that can be reproduced and traced. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there: reproduce first, ask why until you reach the mechanism, no guards that silence crashes, fix the pattern not the instance. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a bug, crash, flaky test, or failure after restart, or when tempted to add a nil check, retry, or try/catch to make an error go away. Reproduce first, ask why until you reach the root cause, fix it there, and fix every instance of the pattern. ([laughnan/agents](https://github.com/laughnan/agents/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when fixing a bug, regression, or recurring operational problem. ([MattiaIppoliti/tia-skills](https://github.com/MattiaIppoliti/tia-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Fixes wrong behaviour where it first went wrong (data model, schema, algorithm, prompt) instead of patching the symptom, lets failures surface as errors, and fixes the whole class, never one instance. Use when fixing a bug, failing test, error or wrong model answer, before adding a guard, fallback, default, retry or special case. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)

- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Debug by reproducing the symptom, tracing the causal chain, and fixing the earliest incorrect assumption or state transition. Use for crashes, flaky behavior, restart failures, and recurring workarounds. ([3metaJun/mstack](https://github.com/3metaJun/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 不具合を調べて直すとき、審査やレビューの指摘を直すときに適用する。症状が出た場所でなく、系が最初に誤った場所まで遡って直す。防御の追加が修正になるのは、扱う状態が正当なときだけ。 ([B16B1RD/bb-writ](https://github.com/B16B1RD/bb-writ/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Debugging. Fix first wrong decision; guards only for legitimate state. ([blackviperxiii-ui/vstack](https://github.com/blackviperxiii-ui/vstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there. Reproduce first, ask why until you reach it, a guard is a fix only for a state that is legitimate. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 调试时应用。将每个症状追溯至系统首次出错的位置，并在那里修复。只有所处理的状态本身合法时，防护判断才算修复。 ([feiandre/HiSkills](https://github.com/feiandre/HiSkills/tree/HEAD/skills/common-skills/principle-skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when a mistake repeats or a routing miss, bad answer, or gate failure shows up a second time. Fix the shared rule, taxonomy, or skill, not the single answer. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 症状を再現して原因までたどり、根本を直す。不具合調査・修正で使う。 ([hasegama/pstack-claude](https://github.com/hasegama/pstack-claude/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack-claude/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to the first place the system did something wrong and fix it there. ([jirayusueb/egm-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/egm-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: When you debug a failure or are about to apply a fix, trace the symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 디버깅할 때 적용. 각 증상을 시스템이 처음 잘못된 곳까지 추적하고 거기서 수정. 가드는 처리하는 상태가 합법적일 때만 수정. ([lee-chance/ai-skills-ko](https://github.com/lee-chance/ai-skills-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack-ko/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Nội quy khi debug: lần mỗi triệu chứng về chỗ đầu tiên hệ thống đi sai và sửa ở đó. Guard chỉ tính là bản sửa khi trạng thái nó xử lý là hợp lệ. ([lichihouse/claude-plugins](https://github.com/lichihouse/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Traces each symptom to the first place the system went wrong and fixes it there. ([NikitaKurpas/agents](https://github.com/NikitaKurpas/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/standards/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply while debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard or fallback is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([Nikshay1/nystack](https://github.com/Nikshay1/nystack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 디버깅할 때 적용한다. 증상마다 시스템이 처음 잘못된 곳까지 거슬러 올라가 거기서 고친다. 가드는 그것이 다루는 상태가 정당할 때만 수정이다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: デバッグ時に適用する。各症状を根本原因までたどり、そこで修正する。まず再現し、根本原因に達するまで「なぜ」を問い、クラッシュを黙らせる nil チェックのガードに抵抗する。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack-skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a failing test, bug, or unexpected behavior (not trivial typos). Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([snooptheone/desu-ai-plugins](https://github.com/snooptheone/desu-ai-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/desustack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-fix-root-causes. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging any failure, or when a fix looks like a guard around a crash rather than a change to the cause. Reproduce first, ask why until you reach the root cause, and fix it there instead of adding a check that only silences the symptom. ([yestheboxer/jig](https://github.com/yestheboxer/jig/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a bug, test failure, crash, or unexpected behavior. Reproduce first, trace the symptom back to the first place the system went wrong, and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Use when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there; reproduce first, ask why until you reach it, resist nil-check guards that silence crashes. ([zakstak/pyre-agent](https://github.com/zakstak/pyre-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-fix-the-bar-first**: WHEN comparing, racing, iterating on or reviewing candidates, existing or new; NOT for a gate that has already failed or for choosing which checks to run; records the selection criteria before evaluation and holds them after results. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-fix-the-bar-first) · ★37)
- **principle-fix-the-latent-issue**: Apply when a request or visible defect may be a downstream symptom. Choose the right problem from the user goal, then fix its causal mechanism. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-fix-the-latent-issue) · 2 copies)
- **principle-flag-your-own-corrections**: Apply whenever you are about to admit fault in any wording — a fact, number, claim, or check you already stated turns out to be wrong, vacuous, premature, or unverified. Say so explicitly rather than quietly citing the corrected value, and treat the admission itself as a reflect trigger. Auto-fires because no regex can enumerate the ways a model admits error. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-flag-your-own-corrections) · ★2)
- **principle-flaky-is-a-bug**: Apply to any intermittent result: a test that passes and fails on the same code, a retry that changed red to green, or a suite that fails only in CI. Find the source of the nondeterminism. Retries and longer timeouts hide it. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-flaky-is-a-bug) · ★1)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★17k · 10k installs · 408 copies)

Settle data shapes and groundwork first, and isolate any state that concurrent actors could change. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★1.6k · 4 copies)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Choose the core data structures, ownership, and sequencing first so downstream logic becomes simpler. Use when before writing logic, especially when state or concurrency is involved. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★117 · 2 copies)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★38)

([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★37)

([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★20 · 7 copies)

([erickb336/sage-stack](https://github.com/erickb336/sage-stack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sage-codex/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious. Use for /principle-foundational-thinking. ([lovrozagar/grunt](https://github.com/lovrozagar/grunt/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★2 · 20 copies)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: 로직을 쓰기 전에 적용한다. 핵심 타입과 자료구조를 고를 때, 스캐폴드와 기능 작업의 순서를 정할 때, 동시에 움직이는 주체들이 무엇을 공유하는지 물을 때. 자료구조를 바르게 잡아 하류 코드가 자명해지게 한다. ([yjb94/jbstack](https://github.com/yjb94/jbstack/tree/HEAD/core/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★2)

- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before non-trivial implementation when code would otherwise commit to an untested shape. ([MattiaIppoliti/tia-skills](https://github.com/MattiaIppoliti/tia-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★1)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: pick core types and data first. ([nodecode-ai/cells](https://github.com/nodecode-ai/cells/tree/HEAD/tools/nodecode-pstack/prose/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★1)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: 処理を書く前に核になる型・データ構造を決めるとき、土台と機能のどちらから作るか決めるとき、並行する処理が同じ状態を共有しそうなときに適用する。 ([B16B1RD/bb-writ](https://github.com/B16B1RD/bb-writ/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))

- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold before features, asking what concurrent actors share. - **principle-foundational-thinking**: 写逻辑前应用：选择核心类型和数据结构、安排骨架与功能的先后、询问并发参与者共享什么。先把数据结构做对，使下游代码自然明确。 ([feiandre/HiSkills](https://github.com/feiandre/HiSkills/tree/HEAD/skills/common-skills/principle-skills/principle-foundational-thinking))

- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply when choosing core types, writing initial logic, or sequencing scaffold and feature work. Get the data shape right so downstream code becomes obvious. ([jirayusueb/egm-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/egm-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Before you write logic, choose the core types and data structures, decide what comes before what, and ask what concurrent actors share, so that the downstream code becomes obvious. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: 로직 작성 전에 적용: 핵심 타입과 데이터 구조 선택, 스캐폴드 대 기능 작업 순서화, 동시 액터가 무엇을 공유하는지 질문. 다운스트림 코드가 명확해지도록 데이터 구조를 올바르게 함. ([lee-chance/ai-skills-ko](https://github.com/lee-chance/ai-skills-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack-ko/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Nội quy trước khi viết logic: chọn kiểu và cấu trúc dữ liệu lõi, thứ tự dựng khung trước hay tính năng trước, các actor song song chia sẻ gì. Cấu trúc dữ liệu đúng thì code sau tự rõ. ([lichihouse/claude-plugins](https://github.com/lichihouse/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Settles core types, data structures, and shared state before writing logic. ([NikitaKurpas/agents](https://github.com/NikitaKurpas/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/standards/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: 로직을 쓰기 전에 적용한다. 핵심 타입과 자료구조를 고르고, 뼈대 작업과 기능 작업의 순서를 정하고, 동시에 움직이는 주체들이 무엇을 공유하는지 묻는다. 자료구조를 제대로 잡으면 그 뒤 코드는 자명해진다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: ロジックを書く前に適用する。コアとなる型とデータ構造の選択、足場と機能の作業順序の決定、並行するアクターが何を共有するかの問い。データ構造を正しくして、下流のコードが自明になるようにする。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack-skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. - **principle-frontmatter**: A seven-line principle whose body starts on line 5. ([EvanBatten/pstack-for-cc](https://github.com/EvanBatten/pstack-for-cc/tree/HEAD/eval/parity/fixtures/skills/principle-frontmatter) · ★2)
- **principle-functional-composition**: Apply whenever writing or reshaping code, in any language. Build behavior from small pure functions composed into pipelines, keep effects at the edges, and write entry points as short orchestrators of reusable modules. ([ja153903/jimmy](https://github.com/ja153903/jimmy/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-functional-composition) · ★1)
- **principle-functional-core**: Apply when writing business logic, or a change that mixes decisions with I/O. Decisions live in pure functions, and a thin shell does the I/O. ([pdevito3/pdstack](https://github.com/pdevito3/pdstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/pdstack/skills/principle-functional-core))
- **principle-functional-programming**: 関数型プログラミングの考え方でコードを設計・実装・レビューするスキル。業務のモデルを型で表し、純粋関数による状態の遷移として処理を組み立て、I/O を端に寄せ、依存はカリー化で注入する。 TypeScript / JavaScript で新しい処理を設計・実装するとき、ドメインモデルや業務フローを型と関数で表したいとき、副作用と純粋なロジックの分け方や依存注入の方法に迷ったとき、既存コードをテストしやすい形に整理したいとき、関数型の観点でコードをレビューしてほしいときは必ずこのスキルを使うこと。 「関数型で書いて」「FP で」「純粋関数にして」「副作用を分けて」「DI どうする？」「ドメインモデルを設計して」といった依頼でも使う。 ([kazukit-dev/dotfiles](https://github.com/kazukit-dev/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/skills/principle-functional-programming))
- **principle-gaps-are-not-passes**: WHEN a check was skipped, unavailable, timed out or returned no result before a verdict; NOT for choosing which checks to run; reports each gap and gives a verified, not verified or inconclusive verdict. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-gaps-are-not-passes) · ★37)
- **principle-gate-before-spend**: Apply when paying for the rest of a plan: a review's later stages, a fan-out's next wave, or a whole-file re-run. Decide with the evidence in hand and spend only what the decision needs. ([jirayusueb/egm-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/egm-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-gate-before-spend))
- **principle-generalize-from-rejection**: Apply when a mechanical gate — a lint, validator, blocked-command guard, or CI check — rejects your output for the same reason twice in one session. Fix the generation pattern producing the violation, not just the specific instance. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-generalize-from-rejection) · ★2)
- **principle-golang-respect-context**: Respect request lifetimes when implementing or reviewing Go operations that accept, propagate, or retain a context. Covers cancellation before work and through dependent calls, including context-bearing wrappers. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-golang-respect-context) · ★1)
- **principle-grade-the-evidence**: Before you pass on any claim, give it one grade from the strength and independence of its evidence, never from venue, citation count, or repetition. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/lab/skills/principle-grade-the-evidence))
- **principle-guard-the-context**: Use when a step needs bulk reading (many files, long logs, large diffs) or hands work to another stage: send the reading to subagents, keep summaries and paths in the main thread, and hand off through files. - **principle-guard-the-context-window**: Apply when context is filling up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents; keep summaries in the main thread, not raw payloads. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-guard-the-context-window) · ★17k · 10k installs · 401 copies)

- **principle-hide-representations**: WHEN a private wire, storage or framework representation crosses a module boundary; NOT for internal types that never leave their module; exposes domain concepts and keeps special-case policy at the edge. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-hide-representations) · ★37)
- **principle-hide-useful-complexity**: Apply when designing modules, interfaces, or abstractions. Hide cohesive complexity behind a small interface that reduces caller knowledge. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-hide-useful-complexity))

- **principle-honest**: Tell the user when they are wrong. Use whenever the user states a belief, makes or proposes a decision, shares a plan or draft, asks for approval or feedback, or when what they ask for conflicts with the evidence. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-honest) · ★1)
- **principle-human-on-the-loop**: Work autonomously and loop the user in only where the work needs their judgment. Use whenever you run a workflow on the user's behalf. ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-human-on-the-loop))
- **principle-humans-approve**: Apply before anything reaches a prospect or changes the brain's context. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-humans-approve) · ★3)
- **principle-i18n**: Internationalization (i18n) & localization (l10n) principles — locale-aware date, time, time-zone correctness (persist UTC in DB, render local), CLDR plural rules, message catalogs (ICU MessageFormat, gettext), translatable string composition (no concatenation), bidirectional & right-to-left layout. Auto-load when adding a new locale, formatting dates/times/numbers/currency for display, persisting timestamps, building message catalogs, composing user-facing strings from variables, working with right-to-left scripts, choosing between backend and frontend locale negotiation, or auditing UI-stri… ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-i18n) · ★3)
- **principle-importance-is-obvious**: Apply when building or changing any UI. Make what matters, what happened, and what to do next obvious without explanation. ([tommyc10/tblock](https://github.com/tommyc10/tblock/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-importance-is-obvious))
- **principle-independent-verdict**: Only a verdict from a context that did not write the code counts — an author checks what it meant to build, not what it built, and an author-verified claim on its own diff is not enough. Apply before trusting any review, CI-green diff, self-certification, or self-verified work on a nontrivial change, including verdicts carried across rebases where the patch may have drifted. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-independent-verdict) · ★3)
- **principle-information-architecture**: How to decide a web app's navigational structure — page hierarchy, where a sub-view lives, how nav is grouped, and how users keep their bearings. The design step that creates structure, upstream of the heuristic audit that checks it. Use when adding a page, deciding tabs-vs-routes, grouping a nav menu, introducing a new section or audience, or reviewing whether a growing area still has a usable map. ([oskarc/AssetHub](https://github.com/oskarc/AssetHub/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-information-architecture) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-integrate-through-review**: Apply when delivering implementation work or deciding whether it is complete. Integrate changes through PRs with central checks and independent review rather than equating local verification with delivery. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-integrate-through-review))
- **principle-intent-and-contracts**: Expose concrete meaning and constraints in names, types, outcomes, values, effects, and documentation; distinguish naming from behavior changes. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-intent-and-contracts) · ★1)
- **principle-interpret-observations**: Preserve evidence meaning when receiving observations, selecting current execution evidence, or calculating operational reports, rates, and coverage. Use for reporting correctness; does not grant authority to settle work or change recovery behavior. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-interpret-observations) · ★1)
- **principle-invest**: The quality bar for user stories — INVEST, vertical slicing, and the 3 Cs — plus the anti-patterns to reject. Other story-time skills apply it. Use when judging whether a story is well formed, or when a story-time skill points here. ([Team-Teddy-Development/story-time](https://github.com/Team-Teddy-Development/story-time/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-invest))
- **principle-investigate-read-only**: WHEN the ask is why, how, review, render, recall or diagnose; NOT for requests that also authorise the change; delivers findings and leaves the subject under study unchanged. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-investigate-read-only) · ★37)
- **principle-isolate-mutable-state**: Apply before writing or delegating work when actors may share files, branches, runtime resources, or other mutable state. Separate independent writers before coordinating unavoidable shared writes. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-isolate-mutable-state))
- **principle-isolate-test-data**: Apply to any test that reads or writes persistent state: databases, queues, files, accounts, caches, third-party sandboxes. Each test must create the data it needs, use identifiers that nobody else uses, and leave nothing that changes the result of another test. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-isolate-test-data) · ★1)
- **principle-judge-with-fresh-eyes**: WHEN a quality or acceptance verdict on finished work is due; NOT for routine self-verification while building; has a context that did not build the work give the verdict, or runs a clean review pass from the inventory and states the limitation. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-judge-with-fresh-eyes) · ★37)
- **principle-justify-the-complexity**: Use when adding or keeping a layer, abstraction, option, mechanism, or second path, or when sequencing a refactor or rewrite. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-justify-the-complexity))
- **principle-keep-gates-traceable**: Apply when proposing blockers, planning work, or reporting completion. Require only gates traceable to accepted requirements or demonstrated safety dependencies. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-keep-gates-traceable))
- **principle-keep-it-simple**: KISS, YAGNI and obvious code: decides whether a feature, option, flag, layer, dependency or abstraction should exist, presents the minimal version first, keeps what stays readable, and lands each change as if the design had planned for it. Use when proposing, designing, scoping, writing, changing or reviewing code, plans or specs. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-keep-it-simple) · ★1)
- **principle-keep-one-source-of-truth**: Apply when you are about to create a second place that holds a fact: a config key or mapping, a cache or on-disk format, a document that describes what code or tests already say, or a field a tool will hold for you. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-keep-one-source-of-truth))
- **principle-keep-secrets-out**: WHEN writing reports, logs, issues, commits, docs, wiki pages or memory, quoting terminal output, or sending data to another system; NOT for refusing authorised credential setup or personal data the task needs; keeps credential values and unneeded personal data out. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-keep-secrets-out) · ★37)
- **principle-keep-the-core-pure**: WHEN domain logic lands in an adapter, handler, job or UI component; NOT for wiring that makes no domain decision; keeps decisions in pure functions behind thin framework wiring. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-keep-the-core-pure) · ★37)
- **principle-keep-tracking-aligned**: Apply when starting, switching, handing off, or completing tracked work. Keep authoritative workflow state, ownership, and relationships aligned with actual progress. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-keep-tracking-aligned))
- **principle-label-the-evidence**: WHEN writing findings, reports, handovers or verification summaries; NOT for deciding which checks to run; marks each claim's epistemic status, evidence level and source locator. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-label-the-evidence) · ★37)
- **principle-label-the-unknowns**: Apply when requirements, plans, test cases, or reports contain gaps, assumptions, or open questions. Every unknown must have a type, a state, an owner, and what it blocks. A bare TBD is not allowed. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-label-the-unknowns) · ★1)
- **principle-layout**: A folder and file layout that shows the design, and one order inside every file. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-layout))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★17k · 10k installs · 408 copies)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1k installs)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply while you restructure code, decide how large a diff should be, or feel the pull toward a new abstraction, an extra layer, or a parameter passed through many functions. Prefer deleting code, and ship the narrowest diff that fixes what was asked. - **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. ([Scino/fstack](https://github.com/Scino/fstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★13)

- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Design state-mutating operations to converge on the same end state no matter how many times they run or where a prior run died.
